                            </i>
                        </p>
                
                        <h4>Constructors</h4>
                        <p>
                            What we can do to address your question is to create a <b>constructor</b>. <br>
                            A <b>constructor in C#</b> is a <b>special method</b> that:
                            has the <b>same name</b> as the <b>class</b>; <b>used</b> to <b>initialize objects</b>; and  <br>
                            <b>does not return values.</b> <br>
                            Hence, whenever we <b>create an object</b> (the instance of the class), the <b>constructor</b> is called. <br>
                            The constructor is a method. So, it needs to have an access modifier. <br>
                            Note that it should have the same name as the class name. <br>
                            So, you can say that is a <b>special method</b> because it has an access modifier, has the same name as the
                            class name, may or may not have parameters that would be defined as initial variables or values of the class
                            members, and does not return any value. <br>
                            <b>
                                There is always at least one constructor in every class. <br>
                                If a class is created without a constructor, the compiler creates a default constructor for that class.
                            </b><br><br>
                            C# constructors are: <br>
                            (1.) Default Constructor <br>
                            (2.) Parametized Constructor <br>
                            (3.) Copy Constructor <br>
                            (4.) Static Constructor <br>
                            (5.) Private Constructor
                        </p>
                
                        <h5>Default Constructor</h5>
                        <p>
                            A <b>default constructor</b> is a <b>constructor</b> that has no <b>parameter.</b> <br>
                            It is a parameterless constructor. <br>
                            The syntax for a <b>default constructor</b> is:
                            <pre>
                                access modifier ClassName()
                                {
                                    statement;
                                }
                            </pre>
                            Let us implement a default constructor <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/default-constructor-1-1.png" alt="Default Constructor: Example 1-1"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/default-constructor-1-2.png" alt="Default Constructor: Example 1-2">
                        </p>
                        
                        <h5>Parametized Constructor</h5>
                        <p>
                            A <b>parametized constructor</b> is a <b>constructor</b> that has at least one <b>parameter.</b> <br>
                            The syntax for a <b>parametized constructor</b> is:
                            <pre>
                                access modifier ClassName(parameter1, parameter2, ...)
                                {
                                statement;
                                }
                            </pre>
                            We shall use parametized constructors frequently in the course. <br>
                            So, we shall create a constructor with the default parameters: teacherName, welcome, student1Name, and student2Name.
                            <br>
                            These parameters will be new parameters that we shall create. Note that the data type of these parameters would be
                            included as we create them. <br>
                            We shall <b>define those parameters inside the constructor</b> by <b>assigning the class members</b> (members in the
                            class) to the <b>parameters inside the constructor</b>. <br>
                            Once we define the constructor in the ComputerScience.cs, when we create the object in the Program.cs; the
                            constructor
                            is called. <br>
                            Then, we can assign arguments and output any result we want. <br>
                            Another thing we need to do: we shall make the ComputerScience class to be public. <br>
                            Going forward, we shall use access modifiers whenever we create our classes. <br>
                            Let us demonstrate what we explained. <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/parametized-constructor-1-1.png" alt="Parametized Constructor: Example 1-1"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/parametized-constructor-1-2.png" alt="Parametized Constructor: Example 1-2"> <br><br>
                        
                            <i>
                                <b>Teacher:</b> Do you know the nice thing about using parametized contructors? <br>
                                <b>Student:</b> I think so. <br>
                                With parametized constructors, we can easily change the names in the main class. <br>
                                <b>Teacher:</b> That is correct. <br>
                                Let's say Kent State University greets students and faculty on the first day of school. <br>
                                We only need to make minor changes to our code.
                            </i> <br><br>
                        
                            This would be: <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/parametized-constructor-2-1.png" alt="Parametized Constructor: Example 2-1"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/parametized-constructor-2-2.png" alt="Parametized Constructor: Example 2-2">
                        </p>
                        
                        <h5>Overloading Constructors</h5>
                        <p>
                            We can <b>overload constructors.</b> <br>
                            This implies that we can create two or more constructors with the same name but different parameters. <br>
                            <u>NOTE:</u> This is based on what I have found out. <br>
                            To <b>overload two or more parametized constructors</b>: <br>
                            (1.) If you use the <b>same parameter data type</b> and the <b>same number of parameters</b> for one constructor, do
                            not use that same parameter data type and number of parameters for the other constructor (overloaded
                            constructor).</b> <br>
                            <u>For example:</u> If you use the string data type for three parameters in the first constructor, do not use the
                            string data type and three parameters for the other constructor. <br>
                            You may use the string and integer data types for three parameters for both constructors (because they are different
                            data types). <br>
                            In addition, you may use the string data type and three parameters for the first constructor, and the string data
                            type but two parameters for the second constructor (because the number of parameters are different). <br><br>
                            (2.) The number of parameters in the constructors must not be the same. <br><br>
                            Let us <b>overload a parametized constructor with another parametized constructor.</b> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/overload-parametized-constructor-1-1.png" alt="Overload Parametized Constructor: Example 1-1"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/overload-parametized-constructor-1-2.png" alt="Overload Parametized Constructor: Example 1-2"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/overload-parametized-constructor-1-3.png" alt="Overload Parametized Constructor: Example 1-3"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/overload-parametized-constructor-1-4.png" alt="Overload Parametized Constructor: Example 1-4"> <br><br>
                            <i>
                                <b>Teacher:</b> Did you notice the functionality of overloaded constructors? <br>
                                We can change the parameters with ease. <br>
                                We can write a short story/poem/drama about KSU among others. <br>
                                <b>Student:</b> What if we wanted the <b>parameter data type</b> and the <b>number of parameters</b> in both
                                constructors to be the same? <br>
                                Can we do that? <br>
                                <b>Teacher:</b> What do you think? <br>
                                <b>Student:</b> I don't know. That's why I asked. <br>
                                <b>Teacher:</b> Yes, we can. <br>
                                But, one of constructors would be a <b>default constructor</b> and the other constructor would be a
                                <b>parametized constructor</b>. <br>
                                The <b>default constructor</b> would have fields (with the same data type), rather than parameters. <br>
                                The <b>parametized constructor</b> would have parameters with the same data type as the fields in the default
                                constructor. <br>
                                Let's do it.
                            </i> <br><br>
                        
                            Let us <b>overload a default constructor with a parametized constructor.</b> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/overload-default-constructor-parametized-constructor-1-1.png" alt="Overload Default Constructor with Parametized Constructor: Example 1-1"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/overload-default-constructor-parametized-constructor-1-2.png" alt="Overload Default Constructor with Parametized Constructor: Example 1-2"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/overload-default-constructor-parametized-constructor-1-3.png" alt="Overload Default Constructor with Parametized Constructor: Example 1-3" style="width: 100%;"> <br><br>
                        
                            <i>
                                <b>Teacher:</b> Do you know something else we can do besides overloading a default constructor and a parametized
                                constructor? <br>
                                <b>Student:</b> No Sir. <br>
                                <b>Teacher:</b> Assume we have the same parameter data type that would perform the same function, we can use a
                                <b>Copy constructor</b>. <br>
                                Let's say we have two teachers, two students, and two courses. <br>
                                The two different teachers will greet the two different students enrolled in two different courses. <br>
                                Both perform the same task, <b>greeting</b> even though they are different entities of the same data
                                type. <br>
                                Rather than overloading a default constructor and a parametized constructor, we can 
                                <b>create a parametized constructor</b> and <b>copy that parametized constructor</b>. <br>
                                Let's see how it works.
                            </i>
                        </p>
                        
                        
                        <h5>Copy Constructor</h5>
                        <p>
                            A <b>copy constructor</b> is a <b>constructor</b> that <b>copies</b> a <b>parameterized constructor.</b> <br>
                            Copy constructors are used when we need to: <br>
                            (1.) Create a new object from an existing object.In other words, if we need new values of the existing fields of a
                            class; a copy constructor is helpful. <br>
                            (2.) Create a constructor of the same parameter data types, but of same or different arguments. <br><br>
                            The syntax for a <b>copy constructor</b> is:
                            <pre>
                                // Parametized constructor
                                access modifier ClassName(parameter1, parameter2, ...) 
                                {
                                statement;
                                }
                                // Copy constructor
                                access modifier ClassName(ClassName newObjectName) 
                                {
                                statement;
                                }
                            </pre>
                            Let us <b>copy a constructor</b> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/copy-constructor-1-1.png" alt="Copy Constructor 1-1"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/copy-constructor-1-2.png" alt="Copy Constructor 1-2"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/copy-constructor-1-3.png" alt="Copy Constructor 1-3"> 
                        </p>
                        
                        <h5>Static Constructor</h5>
                        <p>
                            A <b>static constructor</b> is a <b>constructor</b> that <b>can only be called once</b> when an <b>object is created</b> <br>
                            Similar to a default constructor, it is parameterless. It does not accept parameters. <br>
                            Unlike a default constructor, it has no access modifier. Rather, it is declared with the keyword, <b>static</b>. <br><br>
                            If we declare only a static constructor in a class: <br>
                            When an object is created, the static constructor is called and will execute. <br>
                            If another object is created, the static constructor can no longer be called, and so will not execute. Nothing will
                            execute. <br><br>
                            If we declare both a static constructor and a default constructor in a class: <br>
                            When an object is created, both the static constructor and the default constructor are called, and will execute.
                            <br>
                            If another object is created, only the default constructor will be called and will execute. The static constructor
                            will not be called because it has been called once. <br><br>
                            The syntax for a <b>static constructor</b> is:
                            <pre>
                                static ClassName() 
                                {
                                statement;
                                }
                            </pre>
                            Let us implement a static and a default constructor to show that a static constructor is called only one time <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/static-constructor-1-1.png" alt="Static Constructor 1-1"> <br>
                            <img src="images/AModule1CSharp/static-constructor-1-2.png" alt="Static Constructor 1-2" style="width: 100%;">
                        </p>
                                                
                        <h5>Private Constructor</h5>
                        <p>
                            Whenever a constructor is declared, if the access modifier is not specified; by default, that constructor is
                            private. <br>
                            A <b>private constructor</b> can only be declared by specifying the access modifier of the constructor as
                            <b>private</b>. <br>
                            We shall skip this section for now. <br>
                            We shall focus on creating private fields in the class and accessing those fields using properties. Properties is
                            covered in the next module.
                        </p>
                        
                        <h4>Constructor Chaining</h4>
                        <p>
                            <b>Constructor Chaining</b> is the process of <b>calling a constructor</b> from <b>another constructor</b>. <br>
                            It involves several overloaded constructors. <br>
                            ...
                        </p>
                        
                        
                        <h4>Destructors</h4>
                        <p>
                            A <b>destructor</b> is a <b>special method</b> used <b>only in a class</b> to <b>destroy an object</b> and
                            <b>clean-up when the object is collected by the garbage collector.</b> <br>
                            It is also known as a <b>finalizer</b> <br><br>
                            A destructor: <br>
                            (1.) is preceeded with a tilde sign before the constructor.<br>
                            For example: if the constructor is ComputerScience, the destructor is ~ComputerScience <br><br>
                            (2.) can only be one in a class. <br>
                            A class can have only one destructor. <br><br>
                            (3.) cannot be inherited or overloaded. <br><br>
                            (4.) cannot be called. It is invoked automatically. <br><br>
                            (5.) does not take modifiers. <br><br>
                            (6.) does not have parameters. <br><br>
                            We shall discuss destructors in details when we discuss garbage collection.
                        </p>

                
                </div>

                    <h3>Required Readings</h3>                                      
                    <h3>Properties</h3>
                    <p>
                        A <b>property</b> is typically a <b><i>public</i> class member</b> that can <b>read</b>, <b>write</b>, or
                        <b>compute</b> the <b>value</b> of a <b>private field</b>. <br>
                        Just like a constructor, a property is also a <b>method</b>. <br>
                        So, it needs an access modifier. <br>
                        That access modifier typically, must be public. <br>
                        The <b>accessors</b> are the: <b>get accessor</b> and the <b>set accessor</b>. <br>
                        In other words, the <b>get</b> and <b>set</b> parts of a property are called <b>accessors</b>. <br>
                        As mentioned in the previous module: <b>Encapsulation</b> is the combination of fields, properties, methods, and
                        events of an object in a class(capsule). <br>
                        <b>Encapsulation</b> is also used to <b>hide sensitive data from users.</b> <br>
                        The main objective of <b>using Properties</b> is for <b>encapsulation</b> in terms of
                        <b>hiding sensitive data from users</b>. <br>
                        Recall: that a class member is private by default if we do not specify an access modifier for it. <br>
                        It is also private if we specify the private access modifier for it. <br>
                        If we need to access that private class member/variable outside the class, we have to use a property. <br><br>
                        <b>Properties</b> in C#: <br>
                        (1.) Typically have a <b>public</b> access modifier. <br>
                        <i>
                            However, it is possible to have a <b>protected</b> access modifier for the <b>set accessor</b>. <br>
                            This is known as <a
                                href="https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/programming-guide/classes-and-structs/restricting-accessor-accessibility"
                                target="_blank">Asymmetric Accessor Accessibility</a> <br>
                            For this module: we are only concerned with <b>public accessors.</b>
                        </i> <br><br>
                        (2.) Comprises of the <b>get</b> and <b>set</b> accessors.</b> <br>
                        The <b>get accessor</b> is also known as the <b>getter</b> <br>
                        The <b>set accessor</b> is also known as the <b>setter</b> <br>
                        The <b>get</b> property accessor is used to: <b>read</b>, <b>compute</b>; then <b>return</b> the property
                        value.
                        It <b>gets</b> and <b>returns</b> that <b>private field/class member</b>. <br>
                        The <b>set</b> property accessor is used to <b>perform some data validation</b>, <b>compute</b>; then
                        <b>write</b> or <b>assign</b> that <b>private field/class member</b> to a <b>value</b>. <br>
                        We use the keyword, <b>value</b> as the <b>value</b> being assigned by the <b>set</b> accessor. <br><br>
                        (3.) Can be <b>read-write</b> if it has both the <b>get</b> and <b>set</b> accessors. <br><br>
                        (4.) Can be <b>read only</b> if it has <b>only</b> the <b>get</b> accessor. <br><br>
                        (5.) Can be <b>write only</b> if it has <b>only</b> the <b>set</b> accessor. <br>
                        Write-only properties are mostly used to restrict access to sensitive data. <br><br>
                        (6.) Can be <b>auto-implemented</b>. <br>
                        In this case, the fields in the class are defined with the <b>get</b> and <b>set</b> accessors
                        without any implementation. <br>
                        This case usually omits the parameterized constructor (constructor with parameters). <br><br>
                        (7.) Can be implemented as <b>expression-bodied definitions.</b> <br>
                        In this case, the <a href="https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/operators/lambda-expressions" target="_blank">Lambda Expression</a> &equals;&gt;
                        followed by the expression is used for the <b>get</b> and <b>set</b> accessors. <br>
                        Lambda expression is also referred to as Lambda operator. <br>
                        This case omits the <b>return</b> keyword for the <b>get</b> accessor. <br><br>
                        
                        Let us demonstrate the features and functions of properties. <br>
                        <b>Please review the comments in each example for further explanations.</b> <br>
                        Let us get back to the greeting example. <br>
                        The teacher greets the two students. <br>
                        Each student greets the other. <br><br>
                        Let us begin with the <b>Auto-implemented</b> property. <br>
                        We do not use a constructor with this property. <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/auto-implemented-property-1.png" alt="Auto-implemented Property 1"> <br><br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/auto-implemented-property-2.png" alt="Auto-implemented Property 2"> <br><br>
                        
                        Recall: <br>
                        (1.) Constructor has the same name as the Class. <br>
                        (2.) Class names are uppercase or Pascal-case. <br>
                        (3.) Field names are lowercase. <br><br>
                        In that regard, it is recommended that <b>Property names</b> be <b>uppercase</b> of the <b>first letter</b> of
                        the <b>field names</b> <br>
                        The general syntax for a <b>Property</b> is:
                        
                        <pre>access modifier &emsp; data type &emsp; property name</pre> <br><br>
                        
                        Next is the <b>Expression-bodied</b> property. <br>
                        We use a constructor with this property. <br>
                        The <a href="https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/operators/lambda-expressions" target="_blank">Lambda Expression</a> &equals;&gt; is also used. <br>
                        <i>(Compare <b>Lamda Expression in C#</b> with <b>Arrow Function in JavaScript</b>)</i> <br><br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/expression-bodied-property-1.png" alt="Expression-bodied Property 1"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/expression-bodied-property-2.png" alt="Expression-bodied Property 2"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/expression-bodied-property-3.png" alt="Expression-bodied Property 3"> <br><br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/expression-bodied-property-4.png" alt="Expression-bodied Property 4"> <br><br>
                        
                        Next is the <b>Read-Write</b> property. <br>
                        We use a constructor with this property. <br>
                        The <b>get</b> and <b>set</b> accessors are used. <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/read-write-property-1.png" alt="Read-Write Property 1"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/read-write-property-2.png" alt="Read-Write Property 2"> <br><br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/read-write-property-3.png" alt="Read-Write Property 3"> <br><br>
                        
                        Next is the <b>Read-Only</b> property. <br>
                        We use a constructor with this property. <br>
                        Only the <b>get</b> accessor is used. <br>
                        The <b>set</b> accessor is <u>not</u> used. <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/read-only-property-1.png" alt="Read-Only Property 1"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/read-only-property-2.png" alt="Read-Only Property 2"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/read-only-property-3.png" alt="Read-Only Property 3"> <br><br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/read-only-property-4.png" alt="Read-Only Property 4"> <br><br>

                        Next is the <b>Write-Only</b> property. <br>
                        Only the <b>set</b> accessor is used. <br>
                        The <b>get</b> accessor is not used. <br>
                        Because the <b>set</b> accessor does not return any value, we have to use a public method to return the value of
                        the private fields. <br><br>
                        <i>
                            <b>Student:</b> Can we use a constructor without parameters? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Good question. <br>
                            We cannot use a parameterless constructor because a constructor is called when we create the object. <br>
                            We have to use a void method (method without parameters) that will return the greeting. <br>
                            We need to define that method after the properties. <br>
                            Then, we shall call that method in the main class.
                        </i> <br><br>
                        
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/write-only-property-1.png" alt="Write-Only Property 1"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/write-only-property-2.png" alt="Write-Only Property 2"> <br><br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/write-only-property-3.png" alt="Write-Only Property 3">
                    </p>

                    <h4>Application: Body Mass Index (BMI)</h4>
                    <p>
                        Please review the BMI information on these two government websites [from the Center for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) and the National Heart, Lung, and Blood Institute of the National Institutes of Health (NIH)] <br>
                        <a href="https://www.cdc.gov/healthyweight/assessing/bmi/adult_bmi/index.html" target="_blank">About Adult BMI | Healthy Weight, Nutrition, and Physical Activity | CDC</a> <br>
                        (https://www.cdc.gov/healthyweight/assessing/bmi/adult_bmi/index.html) <br><br>
                        
                        and <br>

                        <a href="https://www.nhlbi.nih.gov/health/educational/lose_wt/BMI/bmicalc.htm" target="_blank">Calculate Your BMI - Standard BMI Calculator</a> <br>
                        (https://www.nhlbi.nih.gov/health/educational/lose_wt/BMI/bmicalc.htm) <br><br>

                        As at today, the 3rd day of September, 2020; the BMI information on both websites are: <br><br>
                        <b>NIH website</b> <br>
                        <a href="https://www.nhlbi.nih.gov/health/educational/lose_wt/BMI/bmicalc.htm" target="_blank">Calculate Your BMI - Standard BMI Calculator</a> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/bodyMassIndex-information-NIH.png" alt="Body Mass Index: NIH"> <br><br>
                        <b>CDC website</b> <br>
                        <a href="https://www.cdc.gov/healthyweight/assessing/bmi/adult_bmi/index.html" target="_blank">About Adult BMI | Healthy Weight, Nutrition, and Physical Activity | CDC</a> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/bodyMassIndex-information-1-CDC.png" alt="Body Mass Index - 1: CDC"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/bodyMassIndex-information-2-CDC.png" alt="Body Mass Index - 2: CDC">
                    </p>

                    <h5>Formula for Calculating Body Mass Index: United States System (Customary System)</h5>
                    <p>
                        $
                        BMI = \left(\dfrac{weight}{height * height}\right) * 703 \\[5ex]
                        weight\:\:is\:\:in\:\:pounds (lb) \\[3ex]
                        height\:\:is\:\:in\:\:inches (in)
                        $
                    </p>

                    <h5>Formula for Calculating Body Mass Index: International System (Metric System)</h5>
                    <p>
                        $
                        BMI = \left(\dfrac{weight}{height * height}\right) \\[5ex]
                        weight\:\:is\:\:in\:\:kilograms (kg) \\[3ex]
                        height\:\:is\:\:in\:\:meters (m) \\[3ex]
                        $
                        If you need help with conversion of units, please review the Tables and Examples in 
                        <a href="https://scholarlycommunication.github.io/Presentations/Projects/ArithmeticAlgebra/MeasurementsUnits.html" target="_blank">Measurements and Units</a> <br><br>
                   
                        <i>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> What do you notice in both websites? <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> They both have the information on the BMI. <br>
                            In addition, the CDC website has information for both Adult BMI and Child & Teen BMI among others. <br>
                            The NIH website has recommendations among others. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> That is correct. What else? <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> They have the BMI calculator. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Did you notice any difference in the calculator on both websites? <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> The NIH website has BMI calculator for both Standard and Metric versions. <br>
                            The CDC website has the BMI calculator for only the Metric version. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Did you notice any issue/concern about the BMI information? <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> Not really. Why? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> So, this is what we are going to do. <br>
                            Let us develop the BMI calculator. <br>
                            That calculator will calculate the BMI. <br>
                            Then, we shall interpret that BMI, and make a recommendation. <br>.
                            The CDC website gave us the formula for calculating the BMI. So, we shall use it. <br>
                            As you can see, our calculator would have to use <b>conditional statements</b> for the interpretation and recommendation. <br>
                            But, here is the issue. <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> Wait, I think I see the issue. <br>
                            The issue is with the domains. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Correct! <br>
                            Anyone having a <b>BM</b>I between $18.5$ and $24.9$ <b>both endpoints included</b> has a <b>Normal weight</b>. <br>
                            This is: Normal weight: [18.5, 24.9]. It is a <b>closed interval</b> (relate with Mathematics). <br>
                            So, the <b>Overweight</b> range for the BMI should be the weight greater than $24.9$, (rather than beginning from $25$) and ending at $29.9$ <br>
                            For the <b>Overweight</b> range, the first endpoint is not included, but the second endpoint is included. <br>
                            This is: Overweight: (24.9, 29.9]. It is a <b>half-open half-closed interval</b> (relate with Mathematics) <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Do you know why this would be a problem if the programmer does not fix these issues before developing the calculator? <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> Yes. Because there would not be any interpretation for anyone whose BMI is between $24.9$ and $25$ <br>
                            But, I do see that the BMI information is for values rounded to one decimal place. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> That is correct. <br>
                            And we shall develop our calculator to round to one decimal place. <br>
                            But, it is important we fix the issues. <br>
                            What if we do not want to round? <br>
                            Look at the two examples on the CDC website. The BMI values were rounded to two decimal places. <br>
                            If we developed our calculator to round to two decimal places with the information on those websites "as is"; then we would not have the interpretation and recommendation for those two examples ($24.98$ and $24.96$) because those values are greater than $24.9$ but less than $25$. <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> That makes sense. <br>
                            So, the last one: <b>Obese</b> should be BMI greater than $29.9$, rather than greater than $30$? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> That is correct. 
                        </i> <br><br>
                        So far, we discussed: <br>
                        (1.) Methods (Functions) in C# Programming <br>
                        (2.) Introduction to Object-oriented Programming (Classes, Constructors, and Objects) in Module 1. <br>
                        (3.) Properties in this module: Module 2. <br><br>
                        
                        Let us apply what we learned so far. <br>
                        We shall develop a console application and a desktop application for the Body Mass Index (BMI) calculator. <br><br>

                        (1.) The program should accept user inputs of: <br>
                        (I.) First name <br>
                        (II.) Last name <br>
                        (III.) Weight <br>
                        (IV. Height <br><br>
                        
                        (2.) The program should output the: <br>
                        (A.) BMI value <br>
                        (B.) Interpretation <br>
                        (C.) Recommendation <br><br>

                        (3.) The program should address user-input errors of non-positive numbers for the weight and the height. <br>
                        Inform the user. <br>
                        Then, exit the program without causing it to crash.
                    </p>

                    <h5>Process for the Console Application</h5>
                    <p>
                        (1.) We shall create a new project: <b>Module2</b> <br>
                        The project name is the namespace: <b>Module2</b> <br>
                        The main class file will be named <b>Program.cs</b> <br><br>
                    
                        (2.) Because the first name and the last name is not needed in the calculation, we shall ask for the user input of
                        first name and last name in the <b>Main method</b> <br><br>
                    
                        (3.) We shall create a class under the project. <br>
                        I prefer creating a class by adding a class (as a separate file) rather than creating the class inside the
                        <b>Program.cs</b>. <br>
                        I like to separate classes. However, do what you prefer. <br>
                        So, we shall add a class and name the class, <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> <br>
                        Please note that the class should be under that project/namespace: <b>Module2</b> <br><br>
                    
                        <i>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> You know something I learned in America...as regards dating? <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> What is it? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> You should not ask a woman her weight. <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> LOL. Is that all you learned? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Another thing is that you should not ask a woman her age. <br>
                            But, a woman can ask a man his age. <br>
                            <b>Student:</b> That is correct. In America, you do not ask a woman her weight or her age. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Is that not double standard? <br>
                            Anyway...B2B (back to business)
                        </i> <br><br>
                    
                        (4.) We shall create two fields: <b>weight</b> and <b>height</b> for our class: <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> <br>
                        Because we shall associate the weight and height with the user's first name and last name, we shall <b>declare</b>
                        these <b>fields</b> as <b>private</b>. <br>
                        Be reminded that even if you do not specify an access modifier for the fields, they are declared as private by default.
                        <br>
                        However, it is good practice to specify access modifiers. <br><br>
                    
                        (5.) We shall create public properties for these private fields so we can access them. <br>
                        Remember that <b>method names in C#</b> should begin with uppercase letter. <br>
                        Property is a special method. <br>
                        So, the properties should be the uppercase beginning letters of the fields. <br><br>
                    
                        (6.) We shall create a parametized constructor that will initialize the class fields. <br>
                        Assign the fields to the constructor parameters. <br><br>
                    
                        (7.) We shall create a method: <b>BodyMassIndexDetails</b> <br>
                        This method is necessary to calculate the BMI. <br>
                        We need to <b>throw an exception</b> to ensure that only positive values are entered for the user input weight and
                        height. <br>
                        Zero and negative values should not be allowed. <br><br>
                        <i>No worries. We shall disuss <b>Exception Handling</b> in details in another module. For now, please learn how to
                            do it in this application.
                        </i> <br><br>
                        In addition, we shall interpret the BMI and make recommendation based on the calculated BMI value. <br>
                        We shall create three more variables here. These variables are local variables. <br>
                        For local variables that is <b>directly</b> calculated from the class fields, we do not need to initialize. <br>
                        However, for <b>local variables</b> that <b>are not directly calculated from the class fields</b>, we 
                        <b>must initialize</b> those variables. <br><br>
                    
                        (8.) Go to the <b>Main</b> method in the <b>Program.cs</b> class and ask for user input of the first name, last
                        name, weight, and height <br>
                        <b>NOTE:</b> <br>
                        We will declare variables for: first name, last name, weight, and height in the <b>Main</b> method. <br>
                        However, we shall use the <b>constructor parameters</b> as the <b>same variables for weight and height</b> <br>
                        In other words: our <b>constructor parameters</b> are: <b>weightPounds</b> and <b>heightInches</b> <br>
                        We shall use that <b>weightPounds</b> and <b>heightInches</b> as our <b>variables</b>. <br>
                        Also: we shall use that <b>weightPounds</b> and <b>heightInches</b> as the <b>arguments</b> when we create the 
                        <b>object</b>. <br><br>
                        <b>Recap:</b> <br>
                        We shall use <b>weightPounds</b> and <b>heightInches</b> and <b>their  data types</b> as the <b>parameters for the constructor</b> in our <b>BodyMassIndex class</b>. <br>
                        We shall use the same <b>weightPounds</b> and <b>heightInches</b> and <b>their  data types</b> when we declare <b>variables</b> in the <b>Main method</b>. <br>
                        We shall still use the same <b>weightPounds</b> and <b>heightInches</b> (without the data types) as our <b>arguments</b> in our <b>object</b>. <br><br> 
                        Create the object: <b>calculateBMI</b> <br>
                        Display your summary and make it look nice. <br>
                        To underline anything in the console window: <br>
                        \x1B[4m is the code to underline. <br>
                        \x1B[0m is the code to end the underline (reset). <br>
                        Use the object to call the method. <br><br>
                    
                        (9.) Test your program for each piece/case. <br>
                        In other words, test your program to ensure that the calculation, interpretation, and recommendation is correct for
                        all the BMI options. <br>
                        You may also test the examples on the CDC website. <br><br>
                    
                        (10.) Test your program to check for user input errors. <br>
                        In other words, test your program for <b>exception handling</b>. <br>
                        <b>Stop Debugging</b> after taking the screenshot. <br><br>
                    
                        Please see the images. <br>
                        Review the comments. <br><br>

                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/consoleApp-BMI-3.png" alt="Console App: BMI-3"> <br>

                    <h5>Process for the Desktop Application</h5>
                    <p>
                        (1.) We shall create a <b>class library</b> named <b>BMIClassLibrary</b>. <br>
                        A <b>class library</b> is a <b>library</b> of <b>predefined classes</b> and/or <b>user-defined classes</b> that
                        programmers can <b>use and reuse</b> to <b>develop applications</b>. <br>
                        The class library does not run on it's own. It is not a standalone application. It is being used/resued to develop
                        applications. These applications could be console applications, desktop applications, web applications, or mobile
                        applications. <br><br>
                    
                        <i>
                            <b>Student:</b> Why not just use the class we created already? <br>
                            Why create a class library? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> The application we just did was a <b>Console Application</b> (works in the console window of any
                            desktop/laptop computer; the executable file, <b>.exe</b> file can be made to run in the console window of any
                            computer) <br>
                            The application we want to do is a <b>Desktop Application</b> (works in any desktop; the executable file,
                            <b>.exe</b> file can be made to run in desktop/laptop computer) <br>
                            Both applications have different interfaces even though they have the same functionality. <br>
                            So, we shall make some changes to our code logic for the desktop application.
                        </i> <br><br>
                    
                        The main reasons for using a class library rather than a class are: <br>
                        (A.) The development of desktop, web, mobile applications for the .NET framweork <br>
                        We can use the same class library to develop desktop, web, and mobile applications for the Windows platform.
                        <br><br>
                        (B.) Reusability. We can use the same class library for any project we want to develop. <br>
                        Some may argue that a class is resuable. However, a class library contains at least a class (one or more classes).
                        <br>
                        Besides, imagine creating many classes for many projects as opposed to creating a single class library for those
                        projects. <br><br>
                    
                        Please see the step-by-step directions in the images. <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-1.png" alt="Class Library: 1"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-2.png" alt="Class Library: 2"> <br><br>
                    
                        We will change the location for the library. It is my preference. I like to have quick access to my projects. <br>
                        Usually, I would not change the Solution name. <br>
                        As noted by the definition, a <b>solution</b> is a <b>container for one or more projects in Visual Studio</b>. <br>
                        At the moment, the Solution name is the same name as the Class Library name. However; because I might reuse this
                        class library for another project, or add more classes to this library for other projects, (and please consider
                        these factors when creating your library); I shall rename the Solution name. <br>
                        So, we will uncheck the box, and rename the Solution name to <b>Module2Library</b> <br>
                        You have the option to keep the Solution name as the Class Library name/ Project name (without unchecking the box);
                        or rename it (by unchecking the box). <br>
                    
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-3.png" alt="Class Library: 3"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-4.png" alt="Class Library: 4" style="width: 100%"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-5.png" alt="Class Library: 5" style="width: 100%"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-6.png" alt="Class Library: 6" style="width: 100%"> <br><br>
                    
                        As you can see, that Class1.cs is just a simple class. It does not have all the libraries that we need. <br>
                        So, we have to delete it, and <b>add a new class</b> under the <b>BMIClassLibrary</b>. <br>
                        We shall name that class the same name we used for the class we created in the Console Application. <br>
                        So, we shall name the class: <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> <br>
                    
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-7.png" alt="Class Library: 7"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-8.png" alt="Class Library: 8"> <br><br>
                    
                        As you can see, the <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> has more libraries than the default <b>Class1.cs</b> <br>
                        Next, we are going to <b>copy our entire code</b> under the <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> in the <b>Console
                            Application</b> and <b>paste it</b> in the <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> in the <b>Class Library</b> <br>
                        We <b>will not copy any code</b> from the <b>Program.cs</b> (<i>Do you now see one of the reasons why it is good to
                            separate your created class: <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> from the System class: <b>Program.cs</b></i>) <br>
                        We shall make changes to the code we copied. <br><br>
                    
                        (2.) We shall declare the class: <b>BodyMassIndex.cs</b> as public (as we did in the Console Application) because we
                        are going to use the class. <br><br>
                    
                        (3.) We shall create the two fields: <b>firstName</b> and <b>lastName</b>. Remember they were not included in what
                        we copied. So, we need to include them. <br>
                        The first name and last name of a person is usually a public information. <br>
                        We shall declare them as <b>public</b> so we can access them outside the class. <br><br>
                    
                        (4.) We shall add two parameters: <b>fName</b> and <b>lName</b>, and assign those two class fields: <b>firstName</b>
                        and <b>lastName</b> to the them (Initialize the class fields similar to what we did in the Console Application).
                        <br><br>
                    
                        (5.) In the Console Application, we used the void method <br>
                        Well, we cannot use the void method here. <br>
                        <u>Reason</u>: The void method does not return values. But, we want the MessageBox to display our values. <br>
                        The MessageBox returns string data type. So, the issue is that C# will not convert void data type to a string data
                        type. This will not work. <br>
                        If we decide to use string method (method that returns only a string data type), it will not work because the BMI is
                        of double data type. <br>
                        If we decide to use double method (method that returns only double data type), what about the first name, last
                        names, interpretation of the BMI, recommendation that are strings? <br>
                        If we decide to use multiple methods that return different data types, then we are making this project
                        cumbersome...<i>sort of killing a fly with a hammer</i> <br>
                        So, what data structure can we use that will return different data types? <br>
                        You probably covered this in the Beginners course: the <b>Tuple</b> data structure. <br>
                        So, we are going to use a method that returns the elements of a tuple. <br>
                        Our tuple shall consist of the double data type for the BMI, the string data type for the interpretBMI, and the
                        string data type for the adviceBMI. <br>
                        The method is named: <b>BodyMassIndexDetails()</b> <br>
                        Please review the code logic in the images and review the comments. <br><br>
                    
                        (6.) We shall <b>append each output in a new line</b> <br>
                        The output includes: first name, last name, weight, height, BMI, interpretation, and recommendation. <br>
                        Because we want to append each output in a new line, we shall be using the <b>StringBuilder class</b> with the
                        <b>AppendLine method</b> <br>
                        This is the documentation for the StringBuilder.AppendLine Method: include the link
                        (https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.text.stringbuilder.appendline?view=netcore-3.1) <br>
                        So, we shall <b>delete the Console.WriteLine() method</b> and use the <b>sb.AppendLine() method</b> <br><br>
                    
                        (7.) We shall override the <b>ToString() method</b> <br>
                        This is necessary for: <br>
                        (A.) Nice/Formatted display of our output. <br>
                        (B.) Keeping all our results in one method. <br>
                        (C.) Returning a string that represents our object so it can be displayed in the any of the Toolbox tools that we
                        use. <br>
                        This is the documentation for the ToString() Method: include the link
                        (https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.object.tostring?view=netcore-3.1) <br>
                        Call the method: <b>BodyMassIndexDetails()</b> <br>
                        Create variables with the same names as the elements <br>
                        Create an object of the <b>StringBuilder()</b> class. <br>
                        Assess these variables(tuple elements) and use them in the StringBuilder() class <br>
                        Return the object with the ToString() method. <br><br>
                    
                    
                        (8.) Next, we have to create the desktop application. <br>
                        For this module, we shall create the <b>Windows Form App (.NET Framework)</b> <br>
                        In a later module, we shall use the <b>Windows Presentation Foundation (WPF)</b> <br>
                        The <b>WPF App (.NET Framework)</b> uses <b>Extensible Markup Language (XAML)</b> to create the interface; and the
                        application developed with it can be published in the Windows Store. <br>
                        But, let us stick with <b>Windows Form App (.NET Framework)</b> for this desktop application. <br>
                        At the moment, the <b>BMIClassLibrary</b> is the <b>Startup Project</b> (indicated by the <b>bold</b> emphasis).
                        <br>
                        Build it. Then, try to run it. <br>
                        The <b>Build succeeded</b>. But, the project did not run. <br>
                        Remember, as mentioned earlier; the class library is not a standalone application. It will not run on it's own. <br>
                        The main thing is that the <b>Build succeeded</b> <br>
                    
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-9.png" alt="Class Library: 9" style="width: 80%"> <br><br>
                    
                        In the <b>Solution Explorer</b> window, <br>
                        We have to <b>add a New Project</b> under the Solution name: <b>Module2Library</b> <br>
                        The new project will be a Windows Form Application for C# <br>
                        We shall name it: <b>BMICalculator</b> <br>
                        Then, we will <b>set it</b> as the <b>Startup Project</b> (indicated by the <b>bold</b> emphasis). <br>
                    
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-10.png" alt="Class Library: 10"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-11.png" alt="Class Library: 11"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-12.png" alt="Class Library: 12"> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/class-library-13.png" alt="Class Library: 13"> <br><br>
                    
                        (9.) Go to the <b>Properties</b> window <br>
                        Change the <b>Text</b> property of the form from <b>Form1.cs</b> to <b>BodyMassIndexApplication.cs</b> and presse
                        the ENTER key on your keyboard. <br>
                        <mark>Leave the <b>(Name)</b> property of the form: <b>Form1</b> <i>as is</i></mark> Do not mess with it. <br>
                        Change only the <b>Text</b> property. <br>

                        <b>Exception Handling</b> <br>
                        <img src="images/AModule2CSharp/exception-BMI-desktop-Application-1.png" alt="Exception BMI Desktop Application: 1">
                        <br>

                            <b>Student:</b> The MessageBox is tiny <br>
                            Can we use another toolbox to increase the output? <br>

                            and change the <b>Name</b> property of that label to <b>lblPounds</b> <br>
                            Similarly, we shall change the <b>Text</b> property of the other label to <b>inches</b> for the unit of the <b>Height</b>
                            and change the <b>Name</b> property to <b>lblInches</b> <br>
                            For the appearance and size, let us use a <b>RichTextBox</b> <br>
                            Just drag it from the Toolbox and drop it in the form <br>
                            Change the <b>Font</b> property to Times New Roman Regular 16 like we did the rest of the objects <br>
                            Adjust the objects to fit in the form. <br>
                            We shall name it: <b>richTextboxOutput</b> <br>
                            Then, we shall make a minor changes in <b>Form.cs</b> <br>
                            We shall modify the codes in <b>Lines #55 and #56</b> and add <b>Line #66</b> <br>
                            Then, Build the Solution. <br>
                            And run it.
                        </i> <br><br>

                <h2>Module 4: Inheritance and Polymorphism</h2>

                <div class="w3-row w3-center w3-border w3-xxlarge">
                    <a href="javascript:void(0)" onclick="openMenu13(event, 'Learn13');" id="myLink13">

                    <h3>Required Readings</h3>
                    <p>
                        There are three primary characteristics/pillars of object-oriented programming: <br>
                        (1.) Encapsulation <br>
                        (2.) Inheritance <br>
                        (3.) Polymorphism
                    </p>
                    <h4>Let's Begin with a Story (Yes, Mr. C tells stories too!)</h4>
                    <p>
                        A teacher, Mr. C with his three students: Student $1$, Student $2$, and Student $3$ <br>
                        The students are dual enrolled high school students. <br>
                        They are bold and smart. <br>
                        They are taking a Computer Science course with Mr. C <br><br>
                        <i>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Today, we shall be discussing <b>Inheritance</b> <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> Is this a Biology class? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Why do you ask? <br>
                            <b>Student $2$:</b> Why do you answer questions with questions? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Typical of Nigerians &#128522; <br>
                            It saves you from getting into trouble. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> I asked because we are studying Genetics. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Interesting. <br>
                            So, we can use some interdisciplinary knowledge from both courses. <br>
                            My first question is: <br>
                            What did you inherit from your parents: Dad, Mom, Grand Dad, Grand Mom (Father's side), Grand Dad, Grand Mom
                            (Mother's side), several generations on both sides, atavism? <br>
                            <b>Student $2$:</b> What do you mean by <b>atavism</b> <br>
                            <b>Student $3$:</b> It's a reappearance of an ancestral trait. <br>
                            For example: Remember those Nigerian parents (both of them are Black) that gave birth to a white baby girl? <br>
                            One of the reasons the doctors gave was dormant white genes which entered both of the female's parents' families
                            long ago. <br>
                            That is atavism. <br>
                            You can read the story online. Wait: here is the <a href="https://www.cbsnews.com/news/white-baby-shocks-black-parents-medically-possible/" target="_blank">link from CBS News:</a>
                            (https://www.cbsnews.com/news/white-baby-shocks-black-parents-medically-possible/) <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> CBS News? LOL <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Hey, do not say what you want to say. <br>
                            This is not politics. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> And what if I do? <br>
                            <b>Student $3$:</b> Some other news sources have it as well. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Okay guys, you have not answered my question. <br>
                            <b>Student $2$:</b> Well, as you can see: I have blonde hair and blue eyes. <br>
                            But surprisingly, my parents have brown hair and brown eyes. <br>
                            Why is that? <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> Your parents have the dominant brown hair and brown eyes genes and the recessive blond hair
                            and blue eyes genes. <br>
                            They passed those recessive genes to you. <br>
                            <b>Student $2$:</b> Interesting. <br>
                            But, if I want my children to have those dominant brown hair and brown eyes genes, what should I do? <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> Let me just invite you to our Biology course so you ask those questions. <br>
                            I am still learning. <br>
                            But, just pray for it. Do not do anything to try to disrupt nature. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Okay, get back on track to my questions. <br>
                            <b>Student $3$:</b> I inherited intelligence from my parents. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> So, we had this argument in class: Is intelligence acquired or inherited? <br>
                            Is it by nature or nature or both? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Intelligence is both nature and nurture though it is more of nurture. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> What are your reasons, Sir? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> You have two Nigerian parents, did not attend school did not have any formal education; yet
                            their children are the best graduating medical students. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> Well, the fact that they did not attend school does not mean that they are not intelligent.
                            Someone who is not literate can still be intelligent. I think intelligence is wisdom. <br>
                            <b>Student $3$:</b> I inherited tallness from my Mom. <br>
                            My Mom is tall and my Dad is of average height. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> I am still waiting for your answer. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> Who? Me? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Yes of course. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> I think I inherited my temperament from my Dad. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Bold. Reactive (not proactive). High activity level. Persistent. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> You got it. <br>
                            But, you do know environmental factors contribute as well. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Yes I know. <br>
                            Just like Intelligence, Temperament is both nature and nurture. However, it is more of nurture. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> Now, back to you, Mr. C <br>
                            What did you inherit from your parents? <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> Oh come on. <br>
                            Can't you see it? <br>
                            Everyone is looking at me. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> Meaning? <br>
                            That I am a very handsome man. <br>
                            <b>Everyone starts smiling/laughing</b> <br>
                            It's good to laugh and be relaxed before we start learning some codes. <br>
                            <b>Student $1$:</b> We are looking at you because you are the professor. <br>
                            <b>Teacher:</b> And because I am very handsome. <br>
                            Alright, B2B... <br>
                            Everyone says: <b>Back to Business</b>
                        </i> <br>
                    
                        So as you can see (based on our discussion), one can inherit traits from the Dad, Mom, Mom and Dad, grandparents, or
                        even ancestral traits. <br><br>
                        So far: <br>
                        In your previous course: the Beginner's course; we discussed <br>
                        (1.) Method/Function <br><br>
                    
                        In the previous modules for this course, we discussed: <br>
                        (1.) A class <br>
                        (2.) Constructor <br>
                        (3.) Property <br>
                    
                        Say that we created a class: Class $A$ with some members: fields, properties, and methods <br>
                        We want to create another class: Class $B$ and <b>use the members</b> from class $A$ and also use additional members
                        <br>
                        Keep in mind that class members includes fields, properties, and methods. <br>
                        Rather than create Class $B$ and define all those members from Class $A$, we can create Class $B$ as a child of
                        Class $A$ so that it <b>inherits</b> the class members from Class $A$ and then create the addtional members for it.
                        <br>
                        We can also create Class $C$ to inherit some of the properties of Class $A$ and also some of the properties
                        of Class $B$ <br>
                        So, you can see it this way: <br>
                        Class $A$ as the parent <br>
                        Class $B$ as the child <br>
                        Class $C$ as the grandchild <br>
                        Remember one inherits the genes of grandparents and great grand parents and so on and so forth. The amount
                        depends on the percentage or fraction. (which you discuss in Genetics). <br>
                        Welcome to Inheritance. <br>
                        <b>Inheritance</b> is the <b>ability</b> of a <b>class</b> to <b>inherit</b> the <b>members</b> of
                        <b>another class</b>. <br>
                        The <b>class whose members are inherited from another class</b> is known as the <b>base class</b> or
                        <b>parent class</b> or <b>superclass</b> <br>
                        The <b>class that inherits the class members from another class</b> is known as the <b>derived class</b> or
                        <b>child class</b> or <b>extended class</b> or <b>subclass</b> <br>
                        So, this is seen as: <br>
                        <b>Base class</b> and <b>Derived class</b> OR <br>
                        <b>Base class</b> and <b>Extended class</b> OR <br>
                        <b>Parent class</b> and <b>Child class</b> OR <br>
                        <b>Superclass</b> and <b>Subclass</b> (<i>Compare to <b>Superset</b> and <b>Subset</b> in <b>Algebra</b></i>) <br>
                        (<i>Please get used to these words because we shall be using them interchangeably.</i>) <br>
                        <b><i>Superclass and Subclass are preferred terms for the Java programming language.</i></b> <br><br>
                        The main purpose of inheritance is to reuse, extend, and modify the class members of a parent class by
                        the child class.<br>
                        It allows one to define a child class that reuses (inherits), extends, or modifies/overrides the behavior of a parent class.
                        <br><br>
                        When a class is derived from a base class, the derived class <b>implicitly</b> gains all the members of the base
                        class, besides its constructors and finalizers. <br>
                        The derived class reuses the code in the base class without having to reimplement it. <br>
                        You can add more members in the derived class. <br>
                        The derived class extends the functionality of the base class. <br>
                        The <b>ancestors</b> of a derived class are the superclasses from which the subclass is derived. 
                    </p>

                    <h5>Notable Notes on C# Inheritance</h5>
                    <p>
                        (1.) The inheritance of a base class from a derived class is achieved using the <b>colon</b> : <br>
                        The syntax is:
                    <pre>
                        public class DerivedClass : BaseClass
                    </pre>
                    (2.) C# <b>supports only a single inheritance</b> of the <b>base class</b>. <br>
                    In other words, C# allows a derived class to have only one direct base class. <br>
                    <b>Single Inheritance</b> is defined as the <b>inheritance</b> of <b>a single direct base class</b> by a <b>derived
                        class</b> <br><br>
                    (3.) C# <b>does not support multiple inheritances</b> of <b>base classes</b>. <br>
                    <b>Multiple Inheritance</b> is defined as the <b>inheritance</b> of <b>more than one base class</b> by a <b>derived
                        class</b> <br><br>
                    (4.) C# supports <b>transitive inheritance</b> <br>
                    This is also known as <b>multilevel inheritance</b> <br>
                    Recall the <a href="https://samuelchukwuemeka.github.io/inequalities/#rulesInequalities" target="_blank">Transitive Rule of
                        Inequalities</a>: <br><br>
                    $
                    (I.)\:\:If\:\: c \lt d \:\:and\:\: d \lt e, \:\:then\:\: c \lt e ...Transitive\:\:Rule \\[3ex]
                    (II.)\:\:If\:\: c \gt d \:\:and\:\: d \gt e, \:\:then\:\: c \gt e ...Transitive\:\:Rule \\[3ex]
                    $
                    and the Transitive Property of Real Numbers: <br>
                    (I.) $If\:\:c = d\:\:and\:\:d = e\:\:then\:\:c = e \\[3ex]$
                    Similarly: <br>
                    If Class $B$ inherits from Class $A$ and <br>
                    Class $C$ inherits from Class $B$, then <br>
                    Class $C$ also inherits from Class $A$. <br>
                    So, you can see it this way: <br>
                    Class $A$ as the parent <br>
                    Class $B$ as the child <br>
                    Class $C$ as the grandchild <br>
                    Because the grandchild inherits from the child who inherits from the parent, the grandchild also inherits from the
                    parent. <br><br>
                    (5.) C# supports <b>polymorphism</b> <br>
                    Literally, <i>poly</i> means <i>many</i> and <i>morphs</i> means <i>forms</i>; so <i>polymorphism</i> means <i>many
                        forms</i> <br>
                    We shall discuss Polymorphism later in this module. <br>
                    For now, just know that two or more child classes can inherit class members from the same parent class. <br><br>
                    (6.) C# <b>supports the inheritance of abstract classes or interfaces</b>. We shall discuss this concept later in the
                    module. <br>
                    If you want a child class to inherit from more than one parent class, then you need to use interfaces. <br><br>
                    (7.) <b>Inheritance is demonstrated</b> by <b>creating an instance of the derived class (object of the derived
                        class)</b> and using that object to perform actions on the base class and the derived class. <br>
                    Typically, we do not demonstrate inheritance by creating an object of the base class. <br><br>
                    (8.) All class members of the base class can be inherited except it's constructors and destructors (finalizers). <br>
                    The child class cannot inherit the constructor of the parent class. Each class must define it's own constructor.
                    <br><br>
                    
                    Let us do some examples. <br>
                    Example $1$: Let us start with <b>Zoology</b> <br>
                    Mixed Dog Breed: <b>Golden Dox</b> <br>
                    The Golden Dox is a mixed breed between the Golden Retriever and the Dachshund <br>
                    It is the mutt/cross breed/mixed breed of a small dog (dachshund) and a large dog (golden retriever) <br>
                    What would you expect? <br><br>
                    
                    Physical features of the Dachshund <br>
                    (1.) Elongated/long body <br>
                    (2.) Short, stubby legs <br>
                    (3.) Long floppy ears <br>
                    (4.) Feisty personality <br><br>
                    
                    Physical features of the Golden Retriever <br>
                    (1.) Medium-length body <br>
                    (2.) Broad head. <br>
                    (3.) Triangle-shaped floppy ears. <br>
                    (4.) Very-friendly personality <br>
                    (5.) Thick muzzles <br><br>
                    
                    Physical features of the Golden Dox (seems like the head of the Golden Retriever and the body of a Dachshund) <br>
                    (1.) Long body (inherited from Dachshund) <br>
                    (2.) Short, stubby legs (inherited from Dachshund) <br>
                    (3.) Floppy ears (inherited from both dogs: Dachshund and Golden Retriever) <br>
                    (4.) Friendly personality (inherited from Golden Retriever) <br>
                    (5.) Broad head (inherited from Golden Retriever) <br>
                    (6.) Long muzzle (<i>add as an extension to the child class</i>) <br><br>
                    
                    Let us write programs that illustrate the inheritance of two golden doxes: one from the dachshund and the other from the
                    golden retriever. <br>
                    Remember that C# supports only single inheritance, hence the reason for using two golden doxes. <br>
                    Also, remember the purpose of inheritance. We shall modify the traits as needed. <br>
                    We are going to have three classes (my preference because I like to separate each program. You may write everything in
                    one program if you wish): the class that has the main method: Main Class (Program.cs); the base class (BaseClass.cs) and
                    the Derived Class (DerivedClass.cs). <br>
                    Please review the images and the comments. <br><br>
